1\leq i\leq n
,
B_{(i)}
is the unique block of
\pi
that contains
i
.

In the context of non-commutative probability, and for commutative independences (exchangeable systems in the terminology of
[
21
]
), one uses Good’s formula to introduce non-commutative cumulants for the corresponding notion of independence at stake. As outlined above, the procedure involves taking independent copies of a random variable. In our case, given a unital complex algebra
\mathcal{A}
equipped with two linear functionals
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{C}
where
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
is unital and
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
tracial, it is possible to build for each
n\geq 1
a cyclic probability space
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},D\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}:\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathbb{C}
, such that, for each
1\leq i\leq n
, we have a state-preserving injection

\iota_{i}:(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})\to(\mathcal{A},\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},D\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}),

and such that the
(\iota_{1}(\mathcal{A}),\ldots,\iota_{n}(\mathcal{A}))
are cyclically free.

In fact, as we will see, cyclic freeness is a particular case of cyclic-conditional freeness for which there is a notion of free product (see
[
10
]
). This permits the construction of cyclically-free copies, and any number of them, of the algebra
\mathcal{A}
in a bigger algebra
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}
. Following
[
21
]
, we define

K_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},\kappa^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}:\mathcal{A}^{\otimes n}\to\mathbb{C},

by for any integer
n\geq 1
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

K_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\frac{1}{n}\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\tilde{a}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\tilde{a}_{n}),\penalty\ \penalty\ K_{n}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}\ldots,a_{n})=\frac{1}{n}D\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\tilde{a}_{1}\cdots\tilde{a}_{n})

where
\tilde{a}_{i}=\xi\cdot\iota_{1}(a_{i})+\cdots+\xi^{n}\cdot\iota_{n}(a_{i})
,
\xi=e^{\frac{2i\pi}{n}}
.

This comes with the following combinatorial characterisation of independence: cyclic freeness with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
is equivalent to the vanishing of mixed
(K_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})_{n\geq 1}
and of mixed
(K_{n}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})_{n\geq 1}
.

The rest of the proof consists in proving that
K_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}=\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
and
K_{n}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}=D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
. The fact that
K_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}=\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
is already contained in the paper of Lehner (see Section 4.4).

The partitioned cumulants
K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
,
\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)
are defined as explained above for partitioned classical cumulants. Namely, given a partition
\pi
and a block
B\in\pi
of
\pi
,
B=\{b_{1}<\cdots<b_{p}\}
, we set for each integer
1\leq i\leq p
,

a_{b_{i}}^{\pi}=\xi_{|B|}\cdot\iota_{b_{1}}(a_{b_{i}})+\xi^{2}_{|B|}\cdot\iota_{b_{2}}(a_{b_{i}})+\cdots+\xi^{p}_{|B|}\cdot\iota_{b_{p}}(a_{b_{i}})

where
\xi_{B}
is a
p^{th}
primitive root of unity and set (see Proposition 2.8 in
[
21
]
),

\displaystyle K_{\pi}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}=(\prod_{B\in\pi}|B|)^{-1}D\tilde{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}^{\pi}\cdots a_{n}^{\pi}).

Now, from Proposition 2.7 in
[
21
]
, the following moments-cumulants formula is in hold, for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}(n)}K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}).

There are three basic facts about the partitioned cumulants of
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
contained in the following proposition.

2.5.1 Proposition
.

(1)

Each
K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}
is cyclically invariant, namely, for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})=K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{c\cdot\pi}(a_{n},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}).

(2)

With
\pi_{1}\in{\rm NC}(\{1,\ldots,m\})
,
\pi_{2}\in{\rm NC}(\{m+1,\ldots,m+n\}
,
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
and
b_{1},\ldots,b_{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{m}
:

K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi_{1}\cup\pi_{2}}(\vec{a},\vec{b})=K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi_{1}}(\vec{a})\cdot\kappa^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{\pi_{2}}(\vec{b})+\kappa^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{\pi_{1}}(\vec{a})\cdot K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi_{2}}(\vec{b}).

(3)

Let
\pi
be a partition with a crossing:
(recall that it means
\exists a<b<c<d,\penalty\ a\sim_{\pi}c,\penalty\ b\sim_{\pi}d
)
then
K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})=0
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from the fact that

D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{n}a_{1}\cdots a_{n-1})

by traciality.
The second one follows from the fact that

D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{m}b_{1}\cdots b_{n})=D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{m})\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(b_{1}\cdots b_{n})+{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{m})\cdot D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(b_{1}\cdots b_{n})

whenever
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})
are cyclic free from
(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
.

For the last point, let
\pi
be a partition with a crossing. Let us prove that
K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})=0
. Using the first and second assertion, we reduce our proof to the irreducible blocs of
\pi
which
contains at least 2 blocks. It is enough to show that the
contribution of those irreducible components are zero. So without loss of generality we may
assume that
\pi
is irreducible with at least two blocks, and (by cyclic invariance) such that the first element and the last element are not in the same bloc. In this case, no block of
\pi
is an interval.
We find that the cumulant equals the expectation of an cyclically alternating
word of random variables
which are centered with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
and with respect to
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
(because of
[
21
, Lemma 2.4]
). Therefore it vanishes: indeed, if we have a cyclically alternating sequence of random variables
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
which are centered with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
and with respect to
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
, we have

D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=0.

∎

By using cyclic invariance and equation (
2
) to “pull out” any nested blocks of a non-crossing partition
\pi\in{\rm NC}(n)
, we compute recursively
K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}
in terms of
(K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{m})_{m<n}
. Because
D\kappa_{\pi}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
also satisfy to the points listed in Proposition
2.5.1
, we get by induction that
K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n}=D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
for any
n\geq 1
;

\displaystyle K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=

\displaystyle D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})-\sum_{\mathbb{1}_{n}\neq\pi\in{\rm CyNC}(n)}K^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})-\sum_{\mathbb{1}_{n}\neq\pi\in{\rm CyNC}(n)}D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n}).

2.6.
Cyclically alternating product of cyclically free variables

In the previous sections, we have introduced cumulants for the cyclic free independence. We now prove formulae for these cumulants when the arguments are products of random variables. We recall first definitions pertaining to type
B
free probablity theory
[
6
]
.
Let
n\geq 1
be an integer. The set of non-crossing partitions of type
B
of
[n]
, denoted
NC^{(B)}(n)
, is the set of all non-crossing partitions of
\{-1<-2<\ldots<-n<1<\ldots n\}
invariant with respect to the inversion map
x\mapsto-x
: this means that
-B\in\pi
for any block
V\in\pi
,
\pi\in{\rm NC}^{(B)}(n)
.
Following
[
14
]
, we denote by
{\rm NCZ}\subset{\rm NC}^{(B)}(n)
the set of non-crossing partitions of type
B
with a
zero block
: a block stable by inversion. The complementary set
{\rm NC}^{(B)}(n)\setminus{\rm NCZ}^{(B)}(n)
is denoted
{\rm NCZ}^{\prime}(n)
:

(21)

{\rm NC}^{(B)}(n)={\rm NCZ}(n)\sqcup{\rm NCZ}^{\prime}(n).

We let
{\rm Abs}:{\rm NC}^{(B)}(n)\to{\rm NC}(n)
be the map that take the image of a non-crossing partition of type
B
by the absolute map
{\rm Abs}:\{-1,\ldots,n\}\to\{1,\ldots,n\}
. The fiber above a non-crossing partition
\pi
by the absolute value map is described as follows. The intersection of
{\rm Abs}^{-1}(\pi)
with
{\rm NCZ}(n)
is in bijection with
\pi
: each block of
\pi
yields an element of
{\rm Abs}^{-1}(\pi)\cap{\rm NCZ}(n)
. The intersection of
{\rm Abs}^{-1}(\pi)
with
{\rm NCZ}^{\prime}(n)
is in bijection with blocks of the Kreweras complement of
\pi
.
As before, we have two ways of defining a multilinear maps over
\mathcal{A}
indexed by type
B
non-crossing partitions given sequences
f_{n},g_{n}:\mathcal{A}^{\otimes n}
,
n\geq 1
.
The first one is given by the following prescriptions:

(22)

\displaystyle|f,g|^{(B)}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=g_{|O|}(a_{O})\prod_{V\neq O\,\in\tilde{\pi}}f_{|V|}(a_{V})

\displaystyle\pi=(\tilde{\pi},O)\in{\rm NCZ}(n),

(23)

\displaystyle|f,g|^{(B)}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\prod_{V\in\tilde{\pi}}f_{|V|}(a_{V})

\displaystyle\pi=(\tilde{\pi},R)\in{\rm NCZ}^{\prime}(n)

and the second one:

(24)

\displaystyle[f,g]^{(B)}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=g_{|O|}(a_{O})\prod_{V\neq O\,\in\tilde{\pi}}f_{|V|}(a_{(V,<_{O})})

\displaystyle\pi=(\tilde{\pi},O)\in{\rm NCZ}(n),

(25)

\displaystyle[f,g]^{(B)}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\prod_{V\in\tilde{\pi}}f_{|V|}(a_{(V,<_{R})})

\displaystyle\pi=(\tilde{\pi},R)\in{\rm NCZ}^{\prime}(n)

Figure 4.

We notice that while we have the equality
g_{n}=[f,g]_{1_{n},1_{n}}
, the values of
f
on
a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}
and on the cyclically permuted words of
a_{1}\otimes\cdots a_{n}
are embodied in
[f,g]
, for any integer
1\leq i\leq n
:

[f,g]_{(1_{n},\{i\})}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=f(a_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\otimes a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i-1}).

The above formulae (
24
) and (
25
) give an alternative way of extending a pair of maps
(f,g)
to the set of non-crossing partition of type
B
. Following the general philosophy that a set is incarnated by the functions of interest that it supports, we may use a different symbol
{\rm CyNC}^{(B)}
for the set of non-crossing partitions of type
B
, but for which we require multiplicative functions (functions that factorizes over the blocks of a partition) to fulfill (
25
) and
(
24
)
.

2.6.1 Proposition
.

For any
\pi\in{\rm CyNCZ}(n)
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}]_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ}(n)\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi\end{subarray}}D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}).

For any
\pi\in{\rm CyNCZ}^{\prime}(n)
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}]_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ}^{\prime}(n),\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNC}^{(B)}(n),\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi\end{subarray}}[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}]_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}).

Proof.

The balanced block of a partition
\pi\in{\rm CyNCZ}
is the block
O\in\pi
for which there exists
i\in[n]
such that
i,-i\in O
. Thus, if
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi
, then the balanced block
O^{\prime}
of
\pi^{\prime}
is contained in the balanced block
O
of
\pi
. This implies that the order
<_{O^{\prime}}
coincides with
<_{O}
on the complementary set of
O
in
[n]
. Hence, for any block
V\neq O\in\pi
:

{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{V,<_{0}})=\sum_{\pi_{V}\in{\rm NC}(|V|)}\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi}(a_{V,<_{O^{\prime}}}).

The first formula follows. For the second equality in the second formula, we remark that
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi
and
\pi\in{\rm CyNCZ}^{\prime}
implies
\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ}^{\prime}
for otherwise a block of
\pi
would contain a pair
-i,i
.
For the first equality, it boils down to the observation that any
\pi^{\prime}\in NCZ^{\prime}
with
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi
, the order
<_{\pi^{\prime}}
is equal to
<_{\pi}
. This will follow from the fact the block
O^{\prime}
of the Kreweras complement of
{\rm Abs}(\pi^{\prime})
such that
\pi^{\prime}=({\rm Abs}(\pi^{\prime}),O^{\prime})
contains the block
O
of the Kreweras complement of
\pi
such that
\pi=({\rm Abs}(\pi),O)
. To prove this we use the fact that
{\rm Abs}({\sf Kr}(\pi))={\sf Kr}(Abs(\pi))
and that
O=Abs(O_{\pi})
,
O^{\prime}={\rm Abs}(O_{\pi^{\prime}})
. Since
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi
implies
Kr(\pi)\geq{\sf Kr}(\pi^{\prime})
, one has
O_{\pi}\subset O_{\pi}^{\prime}
and thus
{\rm Abs}(O_{\pi})\subset{\rm Abs}(O_{\pi^{\prime}})
.
∎

The zeta function of CyNCZ is notated
\zeta_{\rm CyNCZ}:{\rm CyNCZ}^{(2)}\to\mathbb{C}
and its Moebius function
\mu_{\rm NCZ}
. By definition, for any
\pi\in{\rm CyNCZ}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ},\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\pi)

\displaystyle=0\quad{\rm if}\quad\pi^{\prime}\neq\pi,

\displaystyle=1\quad{\rm if}\quad\pi^{\prime}=\pi.

Given a partition into intervals
I=\{I_{1},\ldots,I_{n}\}
of
[p]
and a partition
\pi\in{\rm NCZ}(n)
, we denote by
\hat{\pi}
the partition of
{\rm NCZ}(p)
:

i\sim_{\hat{\pi}}j\Leftrightarrow i\in I_{k},\penalty\ j\in I_{l}\,{\rm and}\,k\sim_{\pi}l.

An interval partition
I
yields an interval partition
\bar{I}=\{I_{1},-I_{1},\ldots,I_{p},-I_{p}\}
in
{\rm NCZ^{\prime}}
.
Notice that
\sigma\vee\bar{I}\in NCZ
whenever
\sigma\in NCZ
and the balanced block is
\bigcup_{k\in O}I_{k}\cup\bigcup_{k\in O}-I_{k}
.

2.6.2 Proposition
.

Let
p\geq 1
be an integer and let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}\in\mathcal{A}
. For any non-crossing partition
\pi\in{\rm NCZ}(p)
:

(26)

D\kappa_{\pi}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{I_{1}},\ldots,a_{I_{n}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\sigma}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})

Proof.

Let
p\geq 1
be an integer and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}\in\mathcal{A}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\sigma}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\sigma\end{subarray}}\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\sigma)D{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ},\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\Big[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\geq\pi^{\prime},\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\sigma)\Big]D{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}).

We set, for any pair of partitions
\pi^{\prime}\leq\hat{\pi}
:

\alpha_{\rm\bar{I}}(\pi^{\prime},\hat{\pi})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\geq\pi^{\prime},\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\sigma)

Notice that for
\alpha_{I}(\pi^{\prime},\hat{\pi})
not to be zero, we must have
\hat{\pi}\geq\bar{I}\vee\pi^{\prime}
. Assume that
\chi\geq\bar{I}\vee\pi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\alpha_{\bar{I}}\star\zeta_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\chi)=\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\leq\hat{\pi}\leq\chi}\alpha_{I}(\pi^{\prime},\hat{\pi})=\sum_{\bar{I}\vee\pi^{\prime}\leq\hat{\pi}\leq\chi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\geq\pi^{\prime},\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\sigma)

The interval
[\bar{I}\vee\pi^{\prime},\chi]
is the image of the map
\{\chi\geq\sigma\geq\pi^{\prime}\}\subset{\rm NCZ}\ni\alpha\mapsto\bar{I}\vee\alpha\in{\rm NCZ}
(which is well-defined) and the second sum is a sum over the fiber of this map at
\hat{\pi}
. Thus,

\alpha_{\bar{I}}\star\zeta_{\rm CyNCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\chi)=\delta(\pi^{\prime},\chi).

Hence, whenever
\bar{I}\vee\pi^{\prime}>\pi^{\prime}
, one has
\alpha_{\bar{I}}\star\zeta_{\rm NCZ}(\pi^{\prime},\chi)=0
. Thus
\alpha_{\bar{I}}(\pi^{\prime},\chi)=0
if
\pi^{\prime}\not\geq\bar{I}
.
This implies:

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma\in{\rm CyNCZ}(p),\\
\sigma\vee\bar{I}=\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\sigma}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ},\\
\bar{I}\leq\pi^{\prime}\leq\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\alpha(\pi^{\prime},\hat{\pi})D{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ},\\
\bar{I}\leq\pi^{\prime}\leq\hat{\pi}\end{subarray}}\mu(\pi^{\prime},\hat{\pi})D{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi^{\prime}\in{\rm CyNCZ}(n),\\
\pi^{\prime}\leq\pi\end{subarray}}\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}).

Because
n\not\sim_{\bar{I}}1
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{\pi^{\prime}}(a_{I_{1}},\ldots,a_{I_{n}})
. We are left with proving:

\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime},\hat{\pi})=\mu_{\rm CyNCZ}({\pi}^{\prime},{\pi}).

It comes from the fact that the operator
\hat{\penalty\ }
is a monotone bijection between
[\hat{\pi}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}]\subset{\rm CyNCZ}
and
[{\pi}^{\prime},{\pi}]\subset{\rm CyNCZ}
. The proof is complete.
∎

The following proposition is a straightforward consequence of the previous one.

2.6.3 Corollary
.

Let
\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}
be cyclically free from
\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\}
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
. The following formula is in hold:

\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(a_{1}b_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}b_{n})=\sum_{\pi\in{\rm CyNC^{(B)}}(n)}[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}]_{\pi}^{(B)}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},D\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}]_{{\sf Kr}(\pi)}^{(B)}(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}).

and with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})=0
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}b_{1}\cdots a_{n}b_{n})=\sum_{\pi\in{\rm CyNC^{(B)}}(n)}\kappa_{\pi}^{(B)}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{{\sf Kr}(\pi)}^{(B)}(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})

When
\delta={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})\neq 0
:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(a_{1}b_{1}\cdots a_{n}b_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in{\rm CyNC^{(B)}}(n)}\kappa_{\pi}^{(B)}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}){\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{{\sf Kr}(\pi)}^{(B)}(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt+\delta\sum_{\pi\in{\rm CyNC}^{(B)}}[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}},0]^{(B)}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}},\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}]^{(B)}_{{\sf Kr}(\pi)}(b_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{n})

2.6.4 Remark
.

If
\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\}
is infinitesimally free from
\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}\}
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},\partial{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
:

\partial\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}b_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}b_{n})=\sum_{\pi\in{\rm NC^{(B)}}(n)}\kappa_{\pi}^{(B)}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\kappa_{{\sf Kr}(\pi)}^{(B)}(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n}).

3.
Cyclic-conditional freeness

We recall cyclic-conditional freeness, to begin with. In a second time, we show how to build from the data of a conditional probability space a cyclic-conditional probability space together with a linear map from the latter to the former and prove that conditional freeness between sub-algebras in the former space implies cyclic-conditional freeness for their images in the latter space.

3.1.
Cyclic independences

3.1.1 Definition

(Cyclic-conditional probability space)
.

A
cyclic-conditional probability space
is a complex unital algebra
(\mathcal{A},1_{\mathcal{A}})
with a triple of linear functionals
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
such that
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
is a conditional probability space (see Definition
1.3.10
) and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
is a
tracial
linear functional.

3.1.2 Definition

(Cyclic-conditional freeness
[
10
]
)
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
be a cyclic-conditional probability space.
A family of sub-algebras
(\mathcal{A}_{i}\subset\mathcal{A}:i\in I)
is
cyclically conditionally free
(
cyclically c-free
for short) in
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
if

(1)

the family
(\mathcal{A}_{i}\subset\mathcal{A}:i\in I)
is conditionally free in
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
,

(2)

and, for any sequence
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
of
\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{A}_{i}
which is
cyclically alternating
(thus
n\geq 2)
and centered with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
,

(27)

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{1})\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{n}).

Let us begin by reminding the link between cyclic-conditional freeness and the other cyclic independences. In an augmented unital algebra
(\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{C}\oplus{\mathcal{I}},1_{\mathcal{A}})
equipped with a unital linear functional
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
and a tracial linear functional
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
, two sub-algebras
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0},\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}\subset{\mathcal{I}}
are
cyclic Boolean
independent
[
2
]
if

(1)

the algebra
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}
are Boolean independent in
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
,

(2)

for any cyclically alternating sequence of elements
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}
in
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}\cup\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}
,

(n\geq 2)
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n}).

Two sub-algebras
\mathcal{A}_{1}\subset\mathcal{I},\mathcal{A}_{2}
are
cyclic monotone
independent
[
11
]
if

(1)

the algebras
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are monotone independent in
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
,

(2)

for any alternating product
(b_{0},a_{1},b_{1}\ldots,a_{n},b_{n})
on elements in
\mathcal{A}_{1}\cup\mathcal{A}_{2}
,
a^{\prime}s\in\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
b^{\prime}s\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(b_{0}a_{1}\cdots a_{n}b_{n})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(b_{1})\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(b_{n-1}){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(b_{n}b_{0})

3.1.3 Proposition

(Proposition 3.2.1, 3.2.2 and 3.2.4 of
[
10
]
)
.

Let us consider a cyclic-conditional probability space
(\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{C}\oplus{\mathcal{I}},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
and two sub-algebras
\mathcal{A}_{1}=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}
which are cyclic-conditionnally free.

\scriptstyle\bullet

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1})={\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{2})=0
for any
a_{1}\in\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0},a_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}
, then
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}
and
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}
are cyclic Boolean independent with respect to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
.

\scriptstyle\bullet

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1})=0,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{2})
for any
a_{1}\in\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0},a_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
then
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
is cyclic monotone independent with respect to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
.

\scriptstyle\bullet

If
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{1})
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{2})
for any
a_{1}\in\mathcal{A}_{1},a_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
then
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
is cyclically free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
.

Another interesting observation is the following one.

3.1.4 Proposition
.

If
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are cyclically conditionally free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{1})

and
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{2})
, then
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are cyclically free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{1}-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}_{2})
.

3.2.
From conditional to cyclic-conditional freeness

We now adapt the proof of Proposition
2.2.2
of the previous section to
produce cyclic-conditional freeness from conditional freeness
. As in (
20
), we consider the unital linear map
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}]:T(\mathcal{A})\to\mathbb{C}
given by, for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

(28)

\displaystyle[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}](a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\sum_{1\leq i(1)<\ldots<i(\ell)\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{i(k)},a_{i(k)+1}\ldots,a_{i(k+1)-1}),

(29)

\displaystyle[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}](1_{T(\mathcal{A})})=1.

with
i(\ell+1)=i(1)
and indices written modulo
n
. Recall that the
\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
are the Boolean cumulants.
For example, with
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}](a_{1}\otimes a_{2}\otimes a_{3})

\displaystyle=\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}\otimes a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3}\otimes a_{1}\otimes a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2}\otimes a_{3}\otimes a_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2}\otimes a_{3})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3}\otimes a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3}).

We now give a more geometric justification to the introduction of the linear form
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}]
. Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
is a
W^{*}
-probability space. Recall that this means
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
is positive and faithful. The Gelfand-Neumark-Segal construction applied to
\mathcal{A}
is faithful and
\mathcal{A}
can thus be identified with a sub-algebra of
B(L^{2}(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}))
, the algebra of bounded linear functional acting on
L^{2}(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
. In this representation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a)=\langle a\Omega,\Omega\rangle_{H}
for some cyclic vector
\Omega\in L^{2}(\mathcal{A})
. Set
H=L^{2}(\mathcal{A})
.
We denote by
p:H\to H
the projection onto
\mathbb{C}\Omega
and set
q=({\rm id}_{H}-p)
the projection onto the hyperplane orthogonal to
\mathbb{C}\Omega
.

In the article
[
18
]
of Kerov, it is proven that if the probability distribution of a random variable
A=A^{*}
is
\mu
, then the inverse Markov-Krein transform of
\mu
has its
n^{th}
moment equal to:

{\rm Tr}_{H}\Big[A^{n}-(qAq)^{n}\Big]

Notice that the trace is well-defined because for any polynomial
Q\in\mathbb{C}[X]
,
Q(A)-Q(qAq)
is a trace-class operator (it has finite rank). This inverse Markov-Krein transform is uniquely determined by
\mu
. The natural multivariate version of the inverse Markov-Krein transform of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
is thus the distribution given, for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
, by

a_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes a_{n}\mapsto{\rm Tr}_{H}\Big[a_{1}\cdots a_{n}-qa_{1}q\cdot qa_{2}q\ldots qa_{n}q\Big]

(here again the trace is well-defined).

3.2.1 Proposition
.

The multi-variate inverse Markov-Krein transform of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
, given by

a_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes a_{n}\mapsto{\rm Tr}_{H}\Big[a_{1}\cdots a_{n}-qa_{1}q\cdot qa_{2}q\ldots qa_{n}q\Big],

is the linear form
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}]:T(\mathcal{A})\to\mathbb{C}
as defined in (
28
).

As we mentioned in Remark
2.3.7
, in the particular case where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
is tracial, this definition of the multi-variate inverse Markov-Krein transform coincides with the one proposed independently by Fujie and Hasebe in
[
16
]
.

Proof.

Decomposing
{\rm id}_{H}=p+q
in

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})={\rm Tr}_{H}\Big[pa_{1}\cdot{\rm id}_{H}\cdot a_{2}\cdot{\rm id}_{H}\cdots{\rm id}_{H}\cdot a_{n}p\Big],

it is easily checked that the Boolean cumulants are in fact given by

\beta^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})={\rm Tr}_{H}\Big[pa_{1}\cdot q\cdot a_{2}\cdot q\cdots q\cdot a_{n}p\Big].

In particular, we have

{\rm Tr}_{H}\Big[a_{1}\cdots a_{n}-qa_{1}q\cdot qa_{2}q\ldots qa_{n}q\Big]=\sum_{1\leq i(1)<\ldots<i(\ell)\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{i(k)},a_{i(k)+1}\ldots,a_{i(k+1)-1})

as wanted.
∎

In the next proposition, given a subset
S\subset\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
T(S)
the algebra generated by
S
in
T(\mathcal{A})
(for the concatenation product
\otimes
).
Recall that given a linear functional
f\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{\mathcal{A}}
it is extended to a linear functional on
T(\mathcal{A})
(and we use the same symbol) by setting, for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

f(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=f(a_{1}\cdots a_{n}).

3.2.2 Theorem
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
be a conditional probability space.
Let
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\subset\mathcal{A}
be two
conditionally free
unital sub-algebras of
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
. Then,
T(\mathcal{A}_{1})
and
T(\mathcal{A}_{2})
are two
cyclic-conditionally free
sub-algebras of the cyclic-conditional probability space
(T(\mathcal{A}),{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])
.

Proof.

A generic element in
T(\mathcal{A})
will be denoted
\overset{\rightarrow}{a}
.
Set
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}:=[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}]-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
. It is clear that
[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}]
is tracial, so in order to prove cyclic-conditional freeness, it suffices to prove that, for any cyclically alternating sequence
(\vec{a}_{1},\ldots,\vec{a}_{N})
(
N\geq 2
) of elements in
T(\mathcal{A}_{1})\cup T(\mathcal{A}_{2})
,

[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}](\vec{a}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\vec{a}_{N})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(\vec{a}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\vec{a}_{N}),

or equivalently, that

\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\vec{a}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\vec{a}_{N})=0.

Due to the definition of
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
, it is possible to identify
1_{\mathcal{A}}
and
1_{T(\mathcal{A})}
in order to move between the different level of the tensor products: we quotient
T(\mathcal{A})
by the relations:

\vec{a}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\vec{b}=\vec{a}\otimes\vec{b},\penalty\ \vec{a},\vec{b}\in T(\mathcal{A}).

By linearity, we can assume that each vector
\vec{a}_{k}
is a pure tensor in some
\mathcal{A}_{j(k)}^{\otimes n_{k}}
, centered with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
.
We may write:

\vec{a}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\vec{a}_{N}=x_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes x_{n},

with
\vec{a}_{k}=\otimes_{i\in I_{k}}x_{i}
for a certain interval partition
[n]=I_{1}\cup\cdots\cup I_{N}
chosen in such a way so that
i\in I_{k}\Rightarrow x_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{j(k)}
with
j(1),\ldots,j(k)\in\{1,2\}
and
j(k)\neq j(k+1)
for
1\leq k\leq N
(with the convention
N+1=1
).
Let us remark that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x_{1}\cdots x_{n})=\sum_{1=i(1)<i(2)\ldots<i(\ell)\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(x_{i(k)},x_{i(k)+1}\ldots,x_{i(k+1)-1}).

It implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\vec{a}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\vec{a}_{N})

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(x_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{n})

\displaystyle=[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}](x_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{n})-{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x_{1}\cdots x_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1<i(1)<i(2)\ldots<i(\ell)\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(x_{i(k)},x_{i(k)+1}\ldots,x_{i(k+1)-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1<i(1)\leq i(\ell)<n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x_{i(1)}\cdots x_{i(\ell)-1})\cdot\beta^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x_{i(\ell)},\ldots,x_{i(1)-1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\beta^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x_{j},x_{j+1}\ldots,x_{i})\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x_{i+1}\cdots x_{j-1}),

where the indices are written modulo
n
. The result follows from Lemma
2.2.3
.
∎

3.2.3 Corollary
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
be a probability space. Let
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}\subset\mathcal{A}
be two free unital sub-algebras. Then,
T(\mathcal{A}_{1})
and
T(\mathcal{A}_{2})
are two sub-algebras of
T(\mathcal{A})
which are cyclically free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}])
.

Proof.

It is a particular case of the previous proposition
3.2.2
for which
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}={\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
.
∎

Before stating the next proposition, we recall that the tensor algebra
T(\mathcal{A})
is a co-augmented algebra with augmentation ideal
\mathcal{I}=\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\mathcal{A}^{\otimes n}
. Given a subset
S\subset\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
T_{0}(S)\subset\mathcal{I}
the sub-algebra of
\mathcal{I}
generated by
S
(with respect to the concatenation product).

3.2.4 Corollary
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a complex algebra equipped with one linear functional
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\subset\mathcal{A}
be two sub-algebras which are
Boolean independent
with respect to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
. Then,
T_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{1})
and
T_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{2})
are two
cyclically Boolean independent
sub-algebras of
\mathcal{I}
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])
.

Proof.

It is the particular case where
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
is vanishing on
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
∎

3.2.5 Corollary
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a complex algebra equipped with one linear functional
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{C}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}\subset\mathcal{A}
be two sub-algebras which are
monotone independent
with respect to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
. Then,
T_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{1}),T_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{2})
are two sub-algebras of
\mathcal{I}
which are
cyclically monotone independent
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])
.

Proof.

It is the case where
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
vanishes on
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and is identically equal to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
on
\mathcal{A}_{2}
.
∎

3.3.
Cumulants for cyclic-conditional freeness

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a unital complex algebra equipped with three linear functionals
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathbb{C}
where
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
are unital and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
is tracial. In this section, we aim to define sequences of cumulants so that the vanishing of the mixed cumulants characterises cyclic-conditional freeness between random variables. Our strategy is to reduce cyclic-conditional freeness to cyclic freeness because we already have at our disposal a set of cumulants for this independence.

Let us consider
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}\subset\mathcal{A}
two unital sub-algebras which are cyclically conditionally free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
. Then the algebras
T(\mathcal{A}_{1}),T(\mathcal{A}_{2})\subset T(\mathcal{A})
are cyclic-conditionally free with respect to the triples
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
(see page
3.2.2
)
and
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])
owing to Theorem
2.2.2
. Proposition
3.1.4

implies
(but at this point, we do not state that the converse is true) that
T(\mathcal{A}_{1})
and
T(\mathcal{A}_{2})
are cyclically free with respect to the pair
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}-[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])
. It might be that

({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}-[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])(1_{T(\mathcal{A})})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})-1\neq 0.

Thus, we might utilize the construction of cumulants for cyclic freeness introduced in Definition
2.4.8
and consider the cyclic free cumulants of
\big({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}-[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}])-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})-1)\cdot[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]\big)
.
The above discussion leads us to the following definition.

3.3.1 Definition

(cyclic-conditional cumulants)
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
be a cyclic-conditional probability space. The
cyclic-conditionally free cumulants
of the triple
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
is a triple of sequences

\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}=(\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n})_{n\geq 1},\penalty\ \kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}=(\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})_{n\geq 1},\penalty\ \kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}=(\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})_{n\geq 1}

where, for each
n\geq 1
:

\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n},\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}\colon\mathcal{A}^{\otimes n}\to\mathbb{C}

and

(1)

\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
and
\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
are the sequences of free cumulants of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
, respectively the conditional cumulants of
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})

(2)

\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
is the sequence inductively defined by, for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

(30)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})+(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{i_{k}},\ldots,a_{i_{k+1}-1})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{i_{k}},\ldots,a_{i_{k+1}-1})=\sum_{\pi\in CyNC(n)}[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}]_{\pi}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}).

The
n^{th}
cumulants
\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
is the restriction to
\mathcal{A}^{\otimes n}\subset T(\mathcal{A})^{\otimes n}
of the
n^{th}
cyclic free cumulant
D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
defined by Definition
2.4.3
from the pair
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
where

D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}:T(\mathcal{A})\to\mathbb{C}

is given by
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}-[{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}]-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})-1)\cdot[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]
, that is to say:

(31)

\begin{split}D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})+(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))&\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{i_{k}},\ldots,a_{i_{k+1}-1})\\
-&\sum_{1\leq\ell\leq n}\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{i_{k}},\ldots,a_{i_{k+1}-1})\end{split}

with indices written modulo
n
, and
i_{\ell+1}=i_{1}
. We view
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}=(D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{n})_{n\geq 0}
as a linear map on the full Fock space (with
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(1_{T(\mathcal{A})})=0
).

3.3.2 Example
.

We expand the above formula at low orders
(1,2
and
3)

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1}))={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-(\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2}))

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\kappa_{1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-\kappa_{1}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-(\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2}))

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}a_{2}a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2},a_{3})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3},a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3}))

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-\Big((\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2},a_{3})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3},a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2},a_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-\kappa_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2}){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3})-\kappa_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{3}){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}a_{2}a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2},a_{3})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3},a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3}))

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-\Big((\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3},a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2},a_{3},a_{1})+\beta_{2}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2},a_{3})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3},a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{3})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2},a_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-\Big({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-(\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2}))

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-(({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}))\Big){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\Big(({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},a_{3})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{3})-(\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1},a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2}))

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{1})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{2})+\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})-\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{2})){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}))\Big){{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{2})

Remarkably, the use of the machinery of Lehner
[
21
]
would have lead to the same definition for cyclic-conditional cumulants.

3.3.3 Theorem
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
be a cyclic-conditional probability space. Consider
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
two unital sub-algebras which are conditionally free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are cyclic-conditionally free with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
;

(2)

\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are cyclic free in
(T(\mathcal{A}),{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
where
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
is given by (
31
);

(3)

the mixed cyclic-conditional free cumulants
\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
vanish.

Proof.

By definition, the cumulants
(D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}:\mathcal{A}^{\otimes n}\to\mathbb{C})_{n\geq 1}
considered in Definition
3.3.1
are the restriction of the cyclic infinitesimal free cumulants

(D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}:T(\mathcal{A})^{\otimes n}\to\mathbb{C})_{n\geq 1}
defined in the cyclic infinitesimal probability space
(T(\mathcal{A}),{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
. In particular, the equivalence between (2) and (3) is a consequence of Theorem
2.4.10
. It remains to prove the equivalence between (1) and (2).

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
. We have already proved this implication.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
. Let us denote by
\mathcal{B}
the algebra generated by
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
in
\mathcal{A}
, and by
\mathcal{C}
the algebra generated by
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
in
T(\mathcal{A})
. Using (
30
),
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
is completely determined on
\mathcal{B}
by
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{\mathcal{C}})
. On the other hand, whenever
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are cyclic infinitesimally free in
(T(\mathcal{A}),{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
, the linear map
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
is completely determined on
\mathcal{C}
by
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{T(\mathcal{A}_{1})},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{T(\mathcal{A}_{1})})
and
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{T(\mathcal{A}_{2})},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{T(\mathcal{A}_{2})})
, or equivalently by
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}})
and
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|_{\mathcal{A}_{2}})
. Consequently, the linear functional
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
is completely determined on
\mathcal{B}
by
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|_{\mathcal{A}_{1}})
and
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|_{\mathcal{A}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}|_{\mathcal{A}_{2}})
. As wanted, whenever
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are cyclic infinitesimally free in
(T(\mathcal{A}),{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
, the joint distribution of
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
with respect to
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
is the same as two cyclic-conditionally free algebras.
∎

3.4.
Cumulants for cyclic Boolean independence

The cyclic Boolean independence between
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}
corresponds to the cyclic c-freeness of
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}
whenever
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{0},\mathcal{A}_{2}^{0}\in\ker({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
. Because
\beta^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
and
\kappa^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
vanish on
\ker({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
, Equation (
30
) reduces to

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{i_{k}},\ldots,a_{i_{k+1}-1})+D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}).

Setting

c_{n}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}):=D\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a_{i},a_{i+1},\ldots,a_{n},a_{1},\ldots,a_{i-1}),

we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n})=c_{n}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq\ell\\
1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{\ell}\leq n\end{subarray}}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(a_{i_{k}},\ldots,a_{i_{k+1}-1}),

which means that
c_{n}
are the cyclic Boolean cumulants defined in
[
2
]
. To state the next proposition, it is necessary to distinguish between
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
and its extension over
T(\mathcal{A})
:

\vec{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}:T(\mathcal{A})\to\mathbb{C},\penalty\ \vec{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})={\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a_{1}\cdots a_{n}).

We denote by
I[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
, where
1\leq a_{1}<\cdots<a_{p}\leq N
the interval partition
\{\llbracket a_{i},a_{i+1}\llbracket,\penalty\ i\in\{0,p+1\}\}
with the convention
a_{0}=1,\penalty\ a_{p+1}=N
. The partition
I(a_{1},\ldots,a_{p})
is the cyclic interval partition obtained from
I[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
by merging
\llbracket 1,a_{1}\llbracket
and
\llbracket a_{p},N\llbracket
.

\vec{x}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\vec{x}_{n}=a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{N}

For readability, we denote by
I[\vec{x}_{1},\ldots,\vec{x}_{N}]
the interval partition with
a_{i}=|\vec{x}_{1}|+\cdots+|\vec{x}_{i}|+1
.
The Boolean cumulants of
\vec{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
can be computed as :

\beta^{\vec{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}}(\vec{x}_{1},\ldots,\vec{x}_{n})=\sum_{I\in{\rm Int}([N]):I\vee I[\vec{x}_{1},\ldots,\vec{x}_{n}]=1_{N}}\prod_{V\in I}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(a_{V}).

3.4.1 Proposition
.

The cyclic Boolean cumulants
c_{n}^{({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}])}
,
n\geq 1
of the pair
({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}])
are given by

c_{n}^{({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}])}(\vec{x}_{1},\ldots,\vec{x}_{N})=\sum_{I(i_{1},\ldots,i_{l})\vee I[\vec{x}_{1},\ldots,\vec{x}_{N}]=1_{N}}\beta_{I(i_{1},\ldots,i_{l})}^{\vec{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}}.

In particular, for any
n\geq 1
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
:

c_{n}^{({\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}])}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n}(a_{i}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}\otimes a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i-1}).

4.
Convolutions and limit theorems

4.1.
Cyclic-conditional additive convolution

In this section and the one that follows we develop the subordination method for computing the distribution of sums and products of cyclically c-free random variables. To that purpose, we assume
\mathcal{A}
to be a
C^{\star}
-algebra, the three linear functionals
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
to be continuous functionals and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
to be positive. For a random variable
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we define the Cauchy transform
G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}:\mathbb{C}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}^{-}
(resp.
G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}:\mathbb{C}^{+}\to\mathbb{C}^{-}
) of
a
with respect to the state
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
(resp. the state
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
) by, for any
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
in the upper-half plane:

G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)={\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(\frac{1}{z-a}),\quad(\textrm{resp. }G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(z)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(\frac{1}{z-a})).

The transform
G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}
and
G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}
are analytic on
\bar{\mathbb{C}}^{+}=\mathbb{C}^{+}\cup\{\infty\}
with the following expansion at the infinity pole:

G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{n})}{z^{n+1}},\quad(\textrm{resp. }G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)=\frac{1}{z}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(a^{n})}{z^{n+1}}).

for any
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
|z|>\|a\|^{-1}
. The Cauchy transform of
a
with respect to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
is an analytic function on
\mathbb{C}^{+}
and takes values in
\mathbb{C}
, besides:

G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(z)=\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})}{z}+\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(a^{n})}{z^{n+1}}.

We notate
\nu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
,
\nu_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
,
\nu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}
the analytic distributions of
a
respectively to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
. Notice that
\nu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
and
\nu_{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
are probability distributions, while
\nu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}
is a signed distribution with total mass
\delta
. Recall the Stieljes inversion formula, as
\varepsilon
tends to
0
:

-\frac{1}{\pi}{{\rm Im}G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}}(x+{\rm i}\varepsilon){dx}\sim\nu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(dx).

The following Lemma confirms that
[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]
is a multivariate extension of the inverse Markov–Krein transform.

4.1.1 Lemma
.

Let
a
be a random variable in the non-commutative probability space
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi})
. The Cauchy transform of
a
with respect to
[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]
is related to the Cauchy transform of
a
with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
by, for any
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
:

(32)

G^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}_{a}(z)=-\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}.

4.1.2 Remark
.

We define the moments generating functional of
a
with respect to
[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]
by, for
z
in the vicinity of
0\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
:

M^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 1}[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}](a^{n})z^{n}

Since
M^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}(z)=\frac{1}{z}G^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}(\frac{1}{z})-1
, the above relation on the Cauchy transforms of
a
with respect to
[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
implies:

M^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}(z)=z\frac{(M^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{1+M^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}.

In fact,

\displaystyle G^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}_{a}(z)=-\frac{d}{dz}{\rm log}(G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z))=-\frac{d}{dz}{\rm log}(\frac{1}{z}(M^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\frac{1}{z})+1))=\frac{1}{z}+\frac{(M^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(\frac{1}{z})}{z^{2}(1+M^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\frac{1}{z}))}

and the desired formula follows.

Proof.

Since both sides of
(
32
)
are analytic, it is enough to prove the equality over
\{z:|z|>\|a\|^{-1}\}
.

To do this observe that

\displaystyle[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}](a^{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq i(1)<\ldots<i(\ell)\leq n}\ \ \prod_{k=1}^{\ell-1}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{i(k+1)-i(k)}(a)\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{i(1)+n-i(\ell)}(a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{j-i})\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{i+n-j}(a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq j\leq n\\
j-i=k\end{subarray}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{k})\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n-k}(a)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}(n-k)\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{k})\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n-k}(a),

from where,

\displaystyle G_{a}^{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}]}(z)-\frac{1}{z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{[{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}](a^{n})}{z^{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{z^{n+1}}\sum_{k=0}^{n}(n-k)\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{k})\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n-k}(a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=k+1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{z^{n+1}}(n-k)\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{k})\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{n-k}(a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{z^{k+m+1}}m\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}(a^{k})\cdot\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{m}(a)

\displaystyle=zG^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{a}(z)\cdot\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{z^{m+1}}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{m}(a)\right)

\displaystyle=zG^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{a}(z)\cdot\left(-\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{z^{m}}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{m}(a)\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=zG^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{a}(z)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{zG^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z)}-1\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=zG^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{a}(z)\cdot\frac{-G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z)-z(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z))^{\prime}}{(zG^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z))^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{z}-\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}.

∎

Recall the definition of
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
in equation (
31
), in the context of a cyclic-conditional probability space
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
.

4.1.3 Lemma
.

For a random variable
a
in a cyclic non-commutative probability space
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
, the Cauchy transform of
a
with respect to
D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
is related to the other Cauchy transforms of
a
by the following relation:

(33)

\displaystyle G^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z)=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(z)+(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}-\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}

Proof.

Linearity of Cauchy transform with respect to the measures.
∎

If
a
and
b
are free with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
, we denote by
\omega_{a}
and
\omega_{a}
the subordination Laurent series of
a
and
b
with respect to
a+b
, in such a way that:

G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)=G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}({\omega}_{a}(z))=G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}({\omega}_{b}(z)).

Since
a
and
b
are infinitesimally free
[
4
]
:

G_{a+b}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)=G_{a}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+G_{b}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z).

By using the previous lemma, we get

\displaystyle G_{a}^{D{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)-(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))}\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))}\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)-(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))\frac{(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}+\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))}\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z),

from which we deduce the following proposition.

4.1.4 Proposition
.

Let
a
and
b
be cyclically conditionally free in
(\mathcal{A},{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega})
. We have

\displaystyle G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(z)=

\displaystyle G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)-(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))\frac{(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}

\displaystyle+\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))}\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+\frac{(G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z))}{G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))}\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)-\frac{(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}.

4.1.5 Remark
.

We specialize the above relations to two cases. We first consider cyclic Boolean independence.
We assume that
\mathcal{A}=\mathbb{C}\oplus\mathcal{I}
with
I={\rm ker}{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
a two-sided ideal and assume further
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})=0
. For any variables
a,b\in\mathcal{I}
:
G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}=G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}=G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)=\frac{1}{z}
. Thus, the subordination series satisfy
\omega_{a}(z)=\omega_{b}(z)=z
and one deduces the following relation from Proposition
4.1.4
:

\displaystyle G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a+b}(z)=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(z)+G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{b}(z)+\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}+\frac{(G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b})^{\prime}(z)}{G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(z)}-\frac{(G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b})^{\prime}(z)}{G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b}(z)}-\frac{1}{z}.

This coincides with Theorem 4.2 in
[
2
]
(with
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{a}(z)=G_{a}(z)-\frac{1}{z}
).

We move on to the cyclic monotone case; the
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
-distribution of
a
is trivial and the
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}
-distribution of
b
is equal to its
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}
-distribution:

\displaystyle G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z)=\frac{1}{z},\quad G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}(z)=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(z),G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(z),

\displaystyle w_{a}(z)=\frac{1}{G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}},\quad w_{b}(z)=z.

Thus, again with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}})=0
, and
F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}:=\frac{1}{G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}
:

G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega^{\prime}_{a}(z)+G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega^{\prime}_{b}(z)=G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z))(F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z))^{\prime}+G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(z)

and

\displaystyle-(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))\frac{(G^{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}_{a+b})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}+\frac{(G_{a}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G_{a}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(\omega_{a}(z))}\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+\frac{(G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z))}{G_{b}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(\omega_{b}(z))}\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)-\frac{(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b}(z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=-\frac{(G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b})^{\prime}(z)}{G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}(z)}+\frac{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}(F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z))(F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z))^{\prime}+\frac{(G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}-\frac{(G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}\circ F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}\circ F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=0.

and wet get:

G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a+b}(z)=G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(z))(F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)+G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{b}(z).

The above formula coincides with Theorem 7.6 of
[
2
]
.

We recall the subordination method for conditional freeness. We have first, with
R_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}
the conditional
R
- transform:

\displaystyle G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z)zG^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(z)-zG^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(z)R_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)).

This implies:

\displaystyle G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}(z)G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))-\omega_{a}(z)G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))R_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z)))

\displaystyle G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega_{b}(z)G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{b}(z))-\omega_{b}(z)G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))=G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))R_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{b}(z)))

We multiply by the
F
transform on both side of the equations

\displaystyle\omega_{a}(z)G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))-F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}(z)G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))=R_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z)))

\displaystyle\omega_{b}(z)G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{b}(z))-F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega_{b}(z)G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))=R_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{b}(z)))

By using the subordinations equations we obtain:

\displaystyle\omega_{a}(z)G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)-F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}(z)G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)=R_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z))

\displaystyle\omega_{b}(z)G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)-F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega_{b}(z)G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)=R_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}|{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z))

We sum the above equations and obtain:

\displaystyle([\omega_{a}(z)-F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))]+[\omega_{b}(z)-F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))])G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)=R_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z))

We substitute in the right-hand side the equation:

R_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z))=(z-F_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z))G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z).

From which we deduce:

\displaystyle[\omega_{a}(z)-F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))]+[\omega_{b}(z)-F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))]=(z-F_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(z))

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\Leftrightarrow\omega_{a}(z)+\omega_{b}(z)-z-(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))+F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z)))=-F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b}(z)

(34)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\Leftrightarrow F^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)-(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))+F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z)))=-F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b}(z).

One gets further

\displaystyle-\frac{(G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}_{a+b}(z)}{G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b}(z)}=\frac{(F_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(z)}{F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a+b}(z)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(F_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)-(\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))+\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z)))}{F^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a+b}(z)-(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))+F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z)))}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))-(\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))+\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z)))}{F^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))-(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))+F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z)))}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)(F_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z))-(\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))+\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z)))}{F^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))-(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))+F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z)))}.

Together with

\displaystyle\frac{(G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(z)}{G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}(z)}=-\frac{\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))}=-\frac{\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)(G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z))}{G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))},

this yields the following expression for the Cauchy transform
G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a+b}
involving only the transforms of
a
and
b
and the free subordination functions:

\displaystyle G_{a+b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}(z)=

\displaystyle G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+G^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z))\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)+(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}(1_{\mathcal{A}}))\frac{\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))}

\displaystyle+\frac{(G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))}{G_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{a}(z))}\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)+\frac{(G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z))}{G_{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}(\omega_{b}(z))}\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle-\frac{\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(F_{a}^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))-(\omega_{a}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a})^{\prime}(\omega_{a}(z))+\omega_{b}^{\prime}(z)(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b})^{\prime}(\omega_{b}(z)))}{F^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))-(F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{a}(\omega_{a}(z))+F^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{b}(\omega_{b}(z)))}.

4.2.
cyclic-conditional multiplicative convolution

The following proposition is a direct corollary of Proposition
2.6.3
and the definition of the cumulants
\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
:

4.2.1 Proposition
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
and
(b_{1},\ldots,b_{n})
be cyclic c-free random variables, then:

\kappa_{n}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}[a_{1}b_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}b_{n}]=\sum_{\pi\in{\rm CyNC}^{(B)}(n)}[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}]^{(B)}_{\pi}(a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})\cdot[\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi}},\kappa^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}\,|\,{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}]_{{\sf Kr}(\pi)}(b_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes b_{n})

We now want to introduce analytical methods to compute the distributions (with respect to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.49,0}\psi},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}\omega}
) of the product
xy
of two cyclic c-free random variables
x
and
y
by utilizing subordination methods. We will prove first subordination formulae for the
\eta
transforms, respectively the infinitesimal
\eta
-transform (both transforms are introduced hereafter) of the random variables
xy
when
x
and
y
are conditionally c-free and
x
and
y
are infinitesimally free, respectively. We define the following formal series, for
x\in\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle M^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z):={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(\frac{zx}{1-zx})=\sum_{n\geq 1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x^{n})z^{n},\quad\eta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)=\frac{M^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)}{1+M^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)},

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x)
is non-zero,
M^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}
is invertible for the composition of series. In this case, the
S
-transform of
x
is defined by
[
38
]
:

\displaystyle\quad S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z):=\frac{1+z}{z}(M^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x})^{\langle-1\rangle}(z).

where we use the superscript
\langle-1\rangle
for the compositional inverse. We recall from
[
38
]
the following, when
x
and
y
are free random variables:

S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{xy}(z)=S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{y}(z).

The author in
[
36
]
introduced the following
infinitesimal
transform, to study the infinitesimal multiplicative convolution:

\displaystyle\partial^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}M_{x}(z):={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}^{\prime}(\frac{1}{1-zx}),\penalty\ \partial^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}\eta_{x}(z):=\frac{\partial^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}M_{x}(z)}{(M^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)+1)^{2}},\penalty\

and finally, when
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}(x)
is non-zero, we set :

\displaystyle\partial^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}S_{x}(z):=-(\partial^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}M_{x}\circ M_{x}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}{}^{\langle-1\rangle})(z)\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}z}M_{x}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}{}^{\langle-1\rangle}(z).

The infinitesimal
S
-transform
\partial^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}S
has the property that when
x
and
y
are infinitesimally free
[
36
]
:

\displaystyle\partial S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{xy}(z)=\partial S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{y}(z)+S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)\partial S^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{y}(z).

We denote by
\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x},\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{y}\in\mathbb{C}[[z]]
the
subordination series for free multiplicative convolution

[
22
]
:

\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{x}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{2n+1}(x,y,\ldots,y,x)z^{n+1},\penalty\ \omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{y}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\beta^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}_{2n+1}(y,x,\ldots,x,y)z^{n+1}

The series
\omega_{x}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
and
\omega_{y}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\varphi}}
are solutions to fixed points equations in the space of formal series:
